Bi-abduction.
We use bi-abduction to automatically create specifications for the data structures. We note that these specifications describe the behaviour of the up to a given bound.
The results are presented in Table
1
. For both languages, we give the number of success and bug specs found (S/B), and the time required to find them. For JavaScript, we additionally include the number of error specs (as JavaScript programs can terminate with a user-thrown error).
It is important to clarify that, for JavaScript, a bug spec means that the program terminated with a native error (e.g. a TypeError or ReferenceError); for C, a bug spec means that the program has thrown an exception (e.g. a segmentation fault).
To obtain bug specs for C, we introduce errors into the code; for JavaScript, the lack of typing information in the language is sufficient on its own.
Finally, we note that the obtained specifications may describe behaviour outside of the use cases of the function and could contain false positive bug reports that need to be treated afterwards either automatically or by the developer.

As for verification, the obtained times for Gillian-JS are slightly slower than those of JaVerT 2.0 due to Gillian being a general framework. We note that they are still quite long, as we have not yet solved the bi-abduction issues of JaVerT 2.0 related to the internal branching of the JavaScript semantics, and view this to be beyond the scope of this paper. The bi-abduction of Gillian-C, on the other hand, yields promising results, as it is able to detect the introduced bugs in very quick times.

7.
Related Work

There is a wide range of works on symbolic analyses for C and JavaScript, such as
(
Kashyap et al. 2014
;
Park and Ryu 2015
;
Jensen
et al. 2009
;
Jourdan et al. 2015
;
Botincan
et al. 2009
)
.
As this paper focusses on the design of Gillian and its associated meta-theory, we centre our discussion on parametric frameworks for obtaining modular symbolic analyses in general, covering the fields of symbolic execution, abstract interpretation, and logic-based analysis and verification.

Symbolic Execution.

Building and maintaining new symbolic execution engines for real-world programming languages is
known to be a daunting task
(
Cadar and Sen 2013
;
Cadar et al. 2011
)
. Researchers in the field have, therefore, tried to automate this process, with tools such
as
Rosette

(
Torlak and
Bodík 2014
;
Torlak and
Bodík 2013
)
and
Chef

(
Bucur
et al. 2014
)
,
which propose two different mechanisms to automatically lift
a user-provided vanilla concrete interpreter for a given target language to a fully-fledged symbolic execution engine for the same language.

More concretely,
Rosette
is an extension of
Racket

(
Racket 2017
)
which additionally provides a set of solver-aided facilities for creating
symbolic values and expressing constraints on those values. With
Rosette
, the concrete interpreter
of the target language is written directly in
Racket
and is then
symbolically interpreted
using

Rosette
’s core symbolic execution engine.
In contrast,
Chef
takes a specially-packaged interpreter as input and executes the target language
programs symbolically by symbolically executing the interpreter’s
binary
.

Importantly, neither
Rosette
nor
Chef
can scale to real-world programming languages.

Rosette
is aimed at domain-specific languages with restricted expressive power, while
Chef
is limited to languages with
“moderately-sized” interpreters.
Interestingly, by implementing a JavaScript symbolic execution engine
natively in OCaml
(
Santos et al. 2019
)
,
instead of running a concrete interpreter on top of
Rosette

(
Santos et al. 2018a
)
,
Fragoso Santos et al. gained a performance speed-up of two orders of magnitude.

Abstract Interpretation.

When it comes to general abstract interpreters, we identify two main strands of work:
those based on small-step semantics and those based on big-step semantics.

Small-Step Abstract Interpreters

Might
(
Might 2010
)
proposes a
methodology
for automatically deriving a family
of sound, computable abstract interpreters from a given concrete interpreter written in
small-step style. This methodology assumes an initial Galois connection
(
Cousot and Cousot 1977
)

between the concrete and abstract domains in order to guarantee the
optimality
of the derived abstract interpreters.
Later, Might and others
(
Horn and Might 2010
;
Horn and Might 2012
)

apply the proposed methodology to a family of abstract machines, obtaining
the so called
abstract abstract machines
, which include expressive programming
language features, such as: first-class control, exception handling, and state.
This technique is further refined in
(
Sergey et al. 2013
)
, where the authors
show how to derive
monadically
-parameterised abstract interpreters from
concrete interpreters. The additional level of parameterisation is used to
capture in a single unified formalism a number of different styles of program analyses,
making it easy to instrument an analysis with parameterisable strategies for improving
precision and performance. In
(
Darais
et al. 2015
)
, the authors propose a library of

Galois tranformers
to streamline the construction of such strategies.
The appeal of this approach is that each Galois transformer can be proved
sound once and for all, making it much easier to prove the soundness of the
obtained analysis.

We observe that, despite offering a general
methodology
for designing
abstract interpreters, none of the works mentioned above automates that methodology,
in that one always has to
manually
follow it to obtain an abstract
interpreter for a given language.
We further note that none of these techniques has been applied to real world
programming languages, such as JavaScript and C.

Big-Step Abstract Interpreters

In
(
Schmidt 1995
)
, Schmidt presents a general approach for
designing abstract interpreters based on co-inductively defined big-step
semantics.
Following similar ideas,
Bodin
et al. 2019
establish a general
framework, the
skeletal semantics
, for developing
concrete and abstract big-step semantics, connected with a general consistency
result, leaving the user to prove a number of simple language-dependent lemmas.
This work, however, has only been applied to a simple While
language with no heap, making its broader applicability
difficult to assess.

Logic-based Analysis and Verification.

\mathbb{K}

(
G. Roşu and T. Florin Şerbănuţă 2010
)
is a language-independent verification infrastructure instantiated to several real-world languages, such as Java, JavaScript, and C
(
Bogdanas and Rosu 2015
;
Park
et al. 2015
;
Hathhorn
et al. 2015
;
Stefanescu et al. 2016
)
, and evaluated on data-structure libraries similar to those of Gillian. As in Gillian, the user of
\mathbb{K}
gets the verification guarantee for free, by construction. Gillian, however, has several advantages over
\mathbb{K}
: Gillian specifications are language-tailored; it supports compositional analyses; and it is faster. For example, the functions of the BST example are verified in
\mathbb{K}
for C in 88.5 seconds and for JavaScript in 25.5 seconds; we verify the same functions in approx. 8 seconds for both languages.

CoreStar

(
Botinčan et al. 2011
)
, was envisioned as a general back-end for tools based on separation logic, where the user would encode the assertions of their language as abstract predicates and provide CoreStar with a separation algebra describing the entailments specific to those predicates. However, CoreStar does not appear to be fit for reasoning about highly complex real-world dynamic languages, such as JavaScript. For instance, in JaVerT
(
Santos et al. 2018b
)
, the authors obtained prohibitive performance even for simple examples.

Viper

(
Müller et al. 2017
;
Müller et al. 2016
)
is also a verification framework designed to
serve as a back-end for tools for permission-based verification, such as separation-logic-based tools.
However,
Silver
, the intermediate language of
Viper
, is not parameterisable. Hence, in order to use

Viper
, users have to encode their memory models in the memory model of
Silver
.
We believe that this approach does not scale well when there is a big mismatch between the memory model
to encode and that of the host language, in this case,
Silver
.

Iris

(
Jung et al. 2015
;
Jung et al. 2018
)
is a Coq-based framework for reasoning about the safety of
concurrent programs. It provides a general methodology for designing new
sound concurrent program logics, as users can encode their own program logics
into Iris and leverage Iris’s soundness result to prove the soundness of their
logics. Iris is, however, mainly aimed at the mechanisation of
meta-theory, while the main goal of Gillian is to streamline the development
of analysis tools.

JaVerT 2.0
(
Santos et al. 2019
)
is a tool for compositional analysis of JavaScript programs, Similarly to Gillian, it supports whole-program symbolic testing, full verification, and
bi-abduction. While the analysis of JaVerT 2.0 is structured modularly, all of its meta-theoretical results, as well as its implementation, are specific to its intermediate language and rely on the specific concrete and symbolic memory models of JavaScript. Gillian takes the highly non-trivial step of generalising both the theory and the implementation of JaVerT 2.0 to a fully language-independent setting.

8.
Conclusions and Further Work

We have presented Gillian, a language-independent framework for the development of compositional symbolic analysis tools, and demonstrated that it can be used to reason about real-world programming languages. Thanks to its parametric meta-theory and modular implementation, Gillian can readily be used by developers to create analysis tools for their language of choice, be it toy, domain-specific, or real-world. We believe that Gillian will be of interest to a broad range of users wishing to obtain correctness guarantees for their code.

The avenues for further work on Gillian are numerous. First of all, our immediate next steps are to streamline the bi-abduction of Gillian-JS and extend the reasoning about the symbolic memory model of Gillian-C to include arbitrary dynamic memory allocation, taking inspiration for the latter from the work of
(
Kirchner et al. 2015
)
done for Frama-C.

We are also investigating ways of extending Gillian with support for reasoning about complex language features, such as events and concurrency, as well as with additional forms of analysis, such as concolic execution. The modular design of Gillian lends itself well to these purposes.

Moreover, the infrastructure of Gillian can be extended to support the analysis of systems running code written in multiple programming languages that inter-operate with each other. A noteworthy goal in this vein of research would be a joint analysis of JavaScript and WebAssembly
(
Haas et al. 2017
)
, the emerging low-level language for the Web.

Finally, we plan to improve Gillian’s error reporting mechanisms and develop interactive tools, such as a trace visualiser and a code-stepper, for the debugging of programs analysed by Gillian, in order to make Gillian more accessible to a wider audience of interested users.

Appendix A
Section 2: Parametric Execution

The Syntax of GIL

v\in\mathcal{V}\triangleq n\in\mathcal{N}\mid i\in\mathbb{Z}\mid s\in\mathcal{S}\mid b\in\mathcal{B}\mid\varsigma\in\mathcal{S}\mkern-2.0mu\mathit{ymb}\mid\tau\in\mathcal{T}\mid f\in\mathcal{F}\mid\overline{v}

e\in\mathcal{E}\triangleq v\mid x\in\mathcal{X}\mid\ominus e\mid e_{1}\oplus e_{2}

c\in\mathcal{C}_{A}

\triangleq

x:=e\mid{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{ifgoto}\,}\,e\ \ i\mid x:=e(e^{\prime})\mid x:=\alpha(e)\mid

\mathit{proc}\in\mathcal{P}roc_{A}

\triangleq

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{proc}\,}\,f(x)\{\overline{c}\}

x:={\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{symb}\!\,}_{j}\mid x:={\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{fresh}\!\,}_{j}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{return}\,}\,e\mid{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{fail}\,}\,e\mid{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{vanish}\,}

\mathtt{p}\in\mathcal{P}rog_{A}

:

\mathcal{F}\rightharpoonup\mathcal{P}roc_{A}

Definition A.1 (State Model).

A state model
S\in\mathbb{S}
is a triple
\langle|S|,V,A\rangle
, consisting of:

(1)
a set of states on which GIL programs operate,
|S|
,
(2)
a set of values stored in those states,
V
, and
(3)
a set of
actions that can be performed on those states,
A
. A state model defines the following functions for acting on states (
\equiv_{pp}
denotes pretty-printing for readability):

•

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setVar}\,}\!:|S|\rightarrow\mathcal{X}\rightarrow V\rightarrow|S|

(
{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setVar}\,}\!(\sigma,x,v)\equiv_{pp}\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setVar}\,}\!(x,v)
)

•

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setStore}\,}\!:|S|\rightarrow(\mathcal{X}\rightharpoonup V)\rightarrow|S|

(
{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setStore}\,}\!(\sigma,\rho)\equiv_{pp}\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setStore}\,}\!(\rho)
)

•

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{store}\,}\!:|S|\rightarrow(\mathcal{X}\rightharpoonup V)

(
{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{store}\,}\!(\sigma)\equiv_{pp}\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{store}\,}\!
)

•

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ee}\,}\!:|S|\rightarrow\mathcal{E}\rightharpoonup V

(
{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ee}\,}\!(\sigma,e)\equiv_{pp}\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ee}\,}\!(e)
)

•

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ea}\,}\!:A\rightarrow|S|\rightarrow V\rightharpoonup\wp(|S|\times V)

(
(\sigma^{\prime},v^{\prime})\in{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ea}\,}\!(\alpha,\sigma,v)\equiv_{pp}\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\alpha\,}(v)\rightsquigarrow(\sigma^{\prime},v^{\prime})
)

A state model
S=\langle|S|,V,A\rangle
is said to be
proper

iff
it defines the following three distinguished actions:
assume
,

symb
, and
fresh
.

GIL Semantic Domains for
S=\langle|S|,V,A\rangle

Call stacks:

\mathit{cs}\in\mathcal{C}\mkern-1.0mus_{S}

\triangleq

|f|\mid|f,x,\rho,i|:\mathit{cs}

where:
f\in\mathcal{F}
,
x\in\mathcal{X}
,
\rho:\mathcal{X}\rightharpoonup V
,
i\in\mathbb{Z}

Configurations:

c\!f\in\mathcal{C}onf_{S}

\triangleq

\langle\mathtt{p},\sigma,\mathit{cs},i\rangle

where:
\mathtt{p}\in\mathcal{P}rog_{A}
,
\sigma\in|S|
,
\mathit{cs}\in\mathcal{C}\mkern-1.0mus_{S}
,
i\in\mathbb{Z}

Outcomes:

o\in\mathcal{O}

\triangleq

\cdot\mid\mathtt{N}(v)\mid\mathtt{E}(v)

where:
v\in V

GIL semantic transitions:
\rightsquigarrow_{S}~:~|S|\times\mathcal{C}\mkern-1.0mus_{S}\times\mathbb{N}\times\mathcal{O}\rightharpoonup\wp(|S|\times\mathcal{C}\mkern-1.0mus_{S}\times\mathbb{N}\times\mathcal{O})
.

Semantics of GIL:
\mathtt{p}\vdash\langle\sigma,\mathit{cs},i\rangle^{o}\,\rightsquigarrow\langle\sigma^{\prime},\mathit{cs}^{\prime},j\rangle^{o^{\prime}}

\frac{{\sf cmd}(\mathtt{p},\mathit{cs},i)=x:=e\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ee}\,}(e)=v}{\mathtt{p}\vdash\langle\sigma,\mathit{cs},i\rangle\,\rightsquigarrow\langle\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setVar}\,}\!(x,v),\mathit{cs},i{+}1\rangle}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{{Assignment}}\frac{{\sf cmd}(\mathtt{p},\mathit{cs},i)=x:=\alpha(e)\hskip 8.50012pt\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ee}\,}(e)=v\hskip 8.50012pt\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\alpha\,}(v)\rightsquigarrow(\sigma^{\prime},v^{\prime})}{\mathtt{p}\vdash\langle\sigma,\mathit{cs},i\rangle\,\rightsquigarrow\langle\sigma^{\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setVar}\,}\!(x,v^{\prime}),\mathit{cs},i{+}1\rangle}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{{Action}}

Definition A.2 (Allocator Model).

An allocator model
AL\in\mathcal{A}L
is a pair
\langle|AL|,V\rangle
consisting of:

(1)
a set
|AL|\ni\xi
of allocators; and

(2)
a set
V
of values to allocate.
It exposes the following function:

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{alloc}\,}:|AL|\rightarrow\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\wp(V)\rightharpoonup|AL|\times V\qquad\quad

which satisfies the well-formedness constraint:

(\xi^{\prime},v)={\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{alloc}\,}(\xi,j,Y)\implies v\in Y.

Logical variables

:

\hat{x}\in\hat{\mathcal{X}}
.

Logical expressions

:

\hat{e}\in\hat{\mathcal{E}}\ \triangleq\ v\mid\hat{x}\in\hat{\mathcal{X}}\mid\ominus\hat{e}\mid\hat{e}_{1}\oplus\hat{e}_{2}
.

Additionally, we write
\pi\in\Pi
to denote logical expressions that that be statically typed as boolean (for example,
{\sf true}
,
{\sf false}
,
\hat{x}\text{~and~}\hat{y}
, etc.).

Definition A.3 (Concrete Memory Model).

A concrete memory model
M\in\mathbb{M}

is a pair
\langle|M|,A\rangle
,
consisting of a set of concrete memories,
|M|\ni\mu
, and a set of actions
A
.
A concrete memory model additionally defines a function
ea
for acting
on memories:

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{\lx@text@underline{ea}}\,}:A\rightarrow|M|\rightarrow\mathcal{V}\rightharpoonup|M|\times\mathcal{V}\qquad\qquad\qquad({{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{\lx@text@underline{ea}}\,}}(\alpha,\mu,v)\equiv_{pp}\mu.\alpha(v))

Definition A.4 (Symbolic Memory Model).

A symbolic memory model
\hat{M}\in\hat{\mathbb{M}}

is a pair
\langle|\hat{M}|,A\rangle
,
consisting of a set of symbolic memories,
|\hat{M}|\ni\hat{\mu}
, and a set of actions
A
.
A symbolic memory model additionally defines a function
\hat{{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{\lx@text@underline{ea}}\,}}
for acting
on memories:

\begin{array}[]{c}\hat{{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{\lx@text@underline{ea}}\,}}:A\rightarrow|\hat{M}|\rightarrow\hat{\mathcal{E}}\rightarrow\Pi\rightharpoonup\wp(|\hat{M}|\times\hat{\mathcal{E}}\times\Pi)\\
((\hat{\mu}^{\prime},\hat{e}^{\prime},\pi^{\prime})\in{\hat{{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{\lx@text@underline{ea}}\,}}}(\alpha,\hat{\mu},\hat{e},\pi)\equiv_{pp}\hat{\mu}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\alpha\,}(\hat{e},\pi)\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\mu}^{\prime},\hat{e}^{\prime},\pi^{\prime}))\end{array}

.

Concrete stores

:

\rho:\mathcal{X}\rightharpoonup\mathcal{V}

Symbolic stores

:

\hat{\rho}:\mathcal{X}\rightharpoonup\hat{\mathcal{E}}

Concrete allocators

:

\xi\in|AL|_{\mathcal{V}}

Symbolic allocators

:

\hat{\xi}\in|AL|_{\hat{\mathcal{E}}}

Mandatory actions

:

A_{0}\triangleq\{{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{assume}\,}\!,{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{fresh}\,}\!,{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{symb}\,}\!\}

Definition A.5 (Concrete State Constructor (
\mathit{CST}
)).

Given an allocator
AL=\langle|AL|,\mathcal{V}\rangle
, the
concrete state constructor
\mathit{CST}:\mathbb{M}\rightarrow\mathbb{S}
is defined as

\mathit{CST}(\langle|M|,A\rangle)\triangleq\langle|S|,\mathcal{V},A\uplus A_{0}\rangle
,
where:

\bullet

|S|=|M|\times(\mathcal{X}\rightharpoonup\mathcal{V})\times|AL|_{\mathcal{V}}

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setVar}\,}\!(\langle\mu,\rho,\xi\rangle,x,v)

\triangleq

\langle\mu,\rho[x\mapsto v],\xi\rangle

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setStore}\,}\!(\langle\mu,\_,\xi\rangle,\rho)

\triangleq

\langle\mu,\rho,\xi\rangle

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{store}\,}\!(\langle\_,\rho,\_\rangle)

\triangleq

\rho

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ee}\,}\!(\langle\_,\rho,\_\rangle,e)

\triangleq

\llbracket e\rrbracket_{\rho}

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ea}\,}\!(\alpha,\langle\mu,\rho,\xi\rangle,v)

\triangleq

\{(\langle\mu^{\prime},\rho,\xi\rangle,v^{\prime})\mid(\mu^{\prime},v^{\prime})={\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{\lx@text@underline{ea}}\,}(\alpha,\mu,v)\}

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{assume}\,}\!(\sigma,v)

\triangleq

\{(\sigma,v)\mid v={\sf true}\}

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{fresh}\,}\!(\langle\mu,\rho,\xi\rangle,i)

\triangleq

\{(\langle\mu,\rho,\xi^{\prime}\rangle,\varsigma)\mid\xi.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{alloc}\,}(i)\,\rightarrow_{\mathcal{S}\mkern-2.0mu\mathit{ymb}}(\xi^{\prime},\varsigma)\}

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{symb}\,}\!(\langle\mu,\rho,\xi\rangle,i)

\triangleq

\{(\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\xi^{\prime}\rangle,v)\mid\xi.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{alloc}\,}(i)\,\rightarrow_{\mathcal{V}}(\xi^{\prime},v)\}

Definition A.6 (Symbolic State Constructor (
\mathit{SST}
)).

Given an allocator
\hat{A}L=\langle|\hat{A}L|,\hat{\mathcal{E}}\rangle
, the
symbolic state constructor
\mathit{SST}:\hat{\mathbb{M}}\rightarrow\mathbb{S}
is defined as

\mathit{SST}(\langle|\hat{M}|,A\rangle)\triangleq\langle\hat{S},\hat{\mathcal{E}},A\uplus A_{0}\rangle
,
where:

\bullet

\hat{S}=|\hat{M}|\times(\mathcal{X}\rightharpoonup\hat{\mathcal{E}})\times|AL|_{\hat{\mathcal{E}}}\times\Pi

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setVar}\,}\!(\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi},\pi\rangle,x,\hat{e})

\triangleq

\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho}[x\mapsto\hat{e}],\hat{\xi},\pi\rangle

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setStore}\,}\!(\langle\hat{\mu},\_,\hat{\xi},\pi\rangle,\hat{\rho})

\triangleq

\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi},\pi\rangle

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{store}\,}\!(\langle\_,\hat{\rho},\_,\_\rangle)

\triangleq

\hat{\rho}

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ee}\,}\!(\langle\_,\hat{\rho},\_,\_\rangle,e)

\triangleq

\hat{\rho}(e)

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ea}\,}\!(\alpha,\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi},\pi\rangle,\hat{e})

\triangleq

\{(\langle\hat{\mu}^{\prime},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi},\pi\,\wedge\pi^{\prime}\rangle,\hat{e}^{\prime})\mid(\hat{\mu}^{\prime},\hat{e}^{\prime},\pi^{\prime})\in\hat{{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{\lx@text@underline{ea}}\,}}(\alpha,\hat{\mu},\hat{e})\}

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{assume}\,}\!(\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi},\pi\rangle,\pi^{\prime})

\triangleq

\{(\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi},\pi\,\wedge\,\pi^{\prime}\rangle,{\sf true})\mid\pi\,\wedge\,\pi^{\prime}\,\text{not {UNSAT}}\}

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{fresh}\,}\!(\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi},\pi\rangle,i)

\triangleq

\{(\langle\mu,\rho,\hat{\xi}^{\prime},\pi\rangle,\varsigma)\mid\hat{\xi}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{alloc}\,}(i)\,\rightarrow_{\mathcal{S}\mkern-2.0mu\mathit{ymb}}(\hat{\xi}^{\prime},\varsigma)\}

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{symb}\,}\!(\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi},\pi\rangle,i)

\triangleq

\{(\langle\mu,\rho,\hat{\xi}^{\prime},\pi\rangle,\hat{x})\mid\hat{\xi}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{alloc}\,}(i)\,\rightarrow_{\hat{\mathcal{X}}}(\hat{\xi}^{\prime},\hat{x})\}

A.1.
While: Syntax, Actions, Compilation to GIL, Concrete/Symbolic memories

While: Syntax, Actions, Memories

\mathit{ws}\in\mathcal{S}\mkern-1.5mu\mathit{tmt}_{\mathbb{W}}

\triangleq

x:=e\mid{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{skip}\,}\mid\mathit{ws}_{1};\mathit{ws}_{2}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{if}\,}(e)\{\mathit{ws}_{1}\}{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{else}\,}\{\mathit{ws}_{2}\}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{while}\,}(e)\{\mathit{ws}\}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{return}\,}\,e\mid x:=f(e)\mid

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{assume}\,}\,e\mid{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{assert}\,}\,e\mid x:=\left\{p_{i}:e_{i}\mid_{i=1}^{n}\right\}\mid x:=e.p\mid e.p:=e^{\prime}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{dispose}\,}\,e

A_{\mathbb{W}}

\triangleq

\{\underline{\sf lookup},\underline{\sf mutate},\underline{\sf dispose}\}

\mu\in M_{\mathbb{W}}

:

\mathcal{S}\mkern-2.0mu\mathit{ymb}\times\mathcal{S}\rightharpoonup\mathcal{V}

\hat{\mu}\in\hat{M}_{\mathbb{W}}

:

\hat{\mathcal{E}}\times\mathcal{S}\rightharpoonup\hat{\mathcal{E}}

While-to-GIL Compiler:
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{W}}:\mathcal{S}\mkern-1.5mu\mathit{tmt}_{\mathbb{W}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\left[\mathcal{C}_{A_{\mathbb{W}}}\right]\times\mathbb{Z}

\frac{}{{\begin{array}[t]{l}\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{W}}(x:=e,\mathtt{pc})\triangleq\\
{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{pc}:x:=e\\
\cdot\leftarrow\mathtt{pc}+1\end{array}}\end{array}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{{Assignment}}\frac{}{{\begin{array}[t]{l}\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{W}}({\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{skip}\,},\mathtt{pc})\triangleq\\

\cdot\leftarrow\mathtt{pc}+1\end{array}}\end{array}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{{Skip}}\frac{}{{\begin{array}[t]{l}\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{W}}({\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{return}\,}\,e,\mathtt{pc})\triangleq\\
{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{pc}:{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{return}\,}\,e\\
\cdot\leftarrow\mathtt{pc}+1\end{array}}\end{array}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{{Return}}\frac{}{{\begin{array}[t]{l}\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{W}}(x:=f(e),\mathtt{pc})\triangleq\\
{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{pc}:x:=f{e}\\
\cdot\leftarrow\mathtt{pc}+1\end{array}}\end{array}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{{Call}}\frac{\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{W}}(\mathit{ws}_{1},\mathtt{pc})=(\overline{c}_{1},\mathtt{pc}_{1})\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{W}}(\mathit{ws}_{2},\mathtt{pc}_{1})=(\overline{c}_{2},\mathtt{pc}_{2})}{{\begin{array}[t]{l}\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{W}}(\mathit{ws}_{1};\mathit{ws}_{2},\mathtt{pc})\triangleq\\
{\begin{array}[]{l}\overline{c}_{1}\\

\cdot\leftarrow\mathtt{pc}_{2}+1\end{array}}\end{array}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{{Sequence}}\frac{\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{W}}(\mathit{ws}_{1},\mathtt{pc}+1)=(\overline{c}_{1},\mathtt{pc}_{1})\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{W}}(\mathit{ws}_{2},\mathtt{pc}_{1}+1)=(\overline{c}_{2},\mathtt{pc}_{2})}{{\begin{array}[t]{l}\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{W}}({\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{if}\,}(e)\{\mathit{ws}_{1}\}{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{else}\,}\{\mathit{ws}_{2}\},\mathtt{pc})\triangleq\\
{\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{pc}:{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{ifgoto}\,}\,(\mathtt{not}\,e)\ \ (\mathtt{pc}_{1}+1)\\
\overline{c}_{1}\\
\mathtt{pc}_{1}:{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{ifgoto}\,}\,{\sf true}\ \ \mathtt{pc}_{2}\\
\overline{c}_{2}\\
\cdot\leftarrow\mathtt{pc}_{2}\end{array}}\end{array}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{{If}}\frac{\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{W}}(\mathit{ws},\mathtt{pc}+1)=(\overline{c},\mathtt{pc}^{\prime})}{{\begin{array}[t]{l}\mathcal{C}_{\mathbb{W}}({\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{while}\,}(e)\{\mathit{ws}\},\mathtt{pc})\triangleq\\

\cdot\leftarrow\mathtt{pc}+1\end{array}}\end{array}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{{Dispose}}

While: Actions in Concrete and Symbolic Memories

\frac{\mu=\_\uplus l.p\mapsto v}{\mu.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\underline{\sf lookup}\,}(\left[l,p\right])\rightsquigarrow(\mu,v)}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{C-Lookup}\\
\frac{\mu=\mu^{\prime}\uplus l.p\mapsto\_\hskip 9.24994pt\mu^{\prime\prime}=\mu^{\prime}\uplus l.p\mapsto v}{\mu.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\underline{\sf mutate}\,}(\left[l,p,v\right])\rightsquigarrow(\mu^{\prime\prime},v)}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{C-Mutate-Present}\\
\frac{(l,p)\notin\mathrm{dom}(\mu)\mu^{\prime}=\mu\uplus l.p\mapsto v}{\mu.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\underline{\sf mutate}\,}(\left[l,p,v\right])\rightsquigarrow(\mu^{\prime},v)}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{C-Mutate-Absent}\\

Appendix B
Section 3: Parametric Soundness

B.1.
Parametric Soundness

Restriction Operators.
A
restriction
operator
\downharpoonleft:X\rightarrow X\rightharpoonup X
on a set
X
,
written
x_{1}\!\downharpoonleft_{x_{2}}
for
\downharpoonleft\!(x_{1},x_{2})
, is a binary associative function
satisfying the following properties:

\frac{}{x\!\downharpoonleft_{x}=x}\quad\textsc{Idempotence}\frac{}{(x_{1}\!\downharpoonleft_{x_{2}})\!\downharpoonleft_{x_{3}}=(x_{1}\!\downharpoonleft_{x_{3}})\!\downharpoonleft_{x_{2}}}\quad\textsc{Right Commutativity}\frac{x_{1}\!\downharpoonleft_{x_{2}\!\downharpoonleft_{x_{3}}}=x}{x_{1}\!\downharpoonleft_{x_{2}}=x_{1}\,\wedge\,x_{1}\!\downharpoonleft_{x_{3}}=x_{1}}\quad\textsc{Weakening}

A restriction operator on states
\downharpoonleft:|S|\rightarrow|S|\rightharpoonup|S|
is said to be

preserved
by a state model
S=\langle|S|,V,A\rangle

if all of the state-generating functions exposed by the state model are monotonic with respect to the pre-order induced by
\downharpoonleft
; put formally:

{\small\frac{}{\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setVar}\,}\!(x,v)=\sigma^{\prime}\implies\sigma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\sigma}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{RMono-SetVar}\frac{}{\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setStore}\,}\!(\rho)=\sigma^{\prime}\implies\sigma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\sigma}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{RMono-SetStore}\frac{}{\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\alpha\,}(v)\rightsquigarrow(\sigma^{\prime},-)\implies\sigma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\sigma}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{RMono-Action}}

We say that
\downharpoonleft
is a restriction operator on a state model

S=\langle|S|,V,A\rangle
, if
\downharpoonleft
is a restriction
operator on the carrier set
|S|
and
\downharpoonleft
is preserved by
S
.
Restriction operators are extended from states to configurations in the standard way:

\langle\mathtt{p},\sigma,\mathit{cs}_{1},i\rangle\!\downharpoonleft_{\langle\mathtt{p},\sigma^{\prime},-,-\rangle}\triangleq\langle\mathtt{p},\sigma\!\downharpoonleft_{\sigma^{\prime}},\mathit{cs}_{1},i\rangle
.

Compatibility.
A pre-order
(X,\leq)
is
compatible
with a restriction operator
\downharpoonleft

on
X

iff
the following properties hold:

\frac{}{x_{1}\!\downharpoonleft_{x_{2}}\,\leq\,x_{1}}\quad\textsc{$\downharpoonleft$-$\leq$ Compatibility}\frac{x_{1}\leq x_{2}}{x_{1}\sqsubseteq x_{2}}\quad\textsc{$\leq$-$\downharpoonleft$ Compatibility}\frac{x_{1}\leq x_{1}^{\prime}\quad x_{2}\sqsubseteq x_{2}^{\prime}}{x_{1}\!\downharpoonleft_{x_{2}}\,\leq\,x_{1}^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{x_{2}^{\prime}}}\quad\textsc{Strengthening}

Soundness Relations.
Given a relation
\sim\,\in X\times Y
between two sets
X
and
Y
, the pre-order
on
X
induced by
\sim
, written
\leq_{\sim}
, is defined as follows:

x_{1}\leq_{\sim}x_{2}
if and only if
\{y\mid x_{1}\,\sim\,y\}\subseteq\{y\mid x_{2}\,\sim\,y\}
.

Definition B.1 (Soundness Relation - States).

Given two state models,
\hat{S}=\langle|\hat{S}|,\hat{V},A\rangle
and
S=\langle|S|,V,A\rangle
,
a soundness relation
\mathit{SR}
for
\hat{S}
in terms of
S
is a triple

\langle\downharpoonleft,\sim_{s},\sim_{v}\rangle
, consisting of:

(1)
a restriction operator
\downharpoonleft
on
\hat{S}
;

(2)
a binary relation
\sim_{s}\,\subseteq|\hat{S}|\times|S|
between
|\hat{S}|
and
|S|
; and

(3)
a ternary relation
\sim_{v}\subseteq|\hat{S}|\times\hat{V}\times V
between
|\hat{S}|
,
\hat{V}
, and
V
,
such that
\downharpoonleft
is compatible with the pre-order induced by
\sim_{s}
(denoted by
\leq
) and the following constraints hold:

\frac{}{\hat{\sigma}\sim_{s}\sigma\implies\hat{\sigma}\vdash\hat{\sigma}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{store}\,}\!\sim_{v}\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{store}\,}\!}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Store}\frac{}{\hat{\sigma}\leq\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\,\wedge\,\hat{\sigma}\sim_{s}\sigma\implies\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}}\vdash\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ee}\,}(e)\sim_{v}\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ee}\,}(e)}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{EvalExpr}\frac{}{{\begin{array}[]{l}\hat{\sigma}\leq\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\,\wedge\,\hat{\sigma}\sim_{s}\sigma\,\wedge\,\hat{\sigma}\vdash\hat{v}\sim_{v}v\\
\hskip 9.24994pt\implies\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setVar}\,}\!(x,\hat{v})\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}}\sim_{s}\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setVar}\,}\!(x,v)\end{array}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{SetVar}\frac{}{{\begin{array}[]{l}\hat{\sigma}\leq\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\,\wedge\,\hat{\sigma}\sim_{s}\sigma\,\wedge\,\hat{\sigma}\vdash\hat{\rho}\sim_{v}\rho\\
\hskip 9.24994pt\implies\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setStore}\,}\!(\hat{\rho})\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}}\sim_{s}\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setStore}\,}\!(\rho)\end{array}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{SetStore}\frac{}{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\alpha\,}(\hat{v})\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime},\hat{v}^{\prime})\,\wedge\,\hat{\sigma}\leq\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}}\,\wedge\,\ \hat{\sigma}\sim_{s}\sigma\,\wedge\,\hat{\sigma}\vdash\hat{v}\sim_{v}v\\
\hskip 18.49988pt\implies\exists\,\sigma^{\prime},v^{\prime}\,.\,\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\alpha\,}(v)\rightsquigarrow(\sigma^{\prime},v^{\prime})\,\wedge\,\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}}\sim_{s}\sigma^{\prime}\,\wedge\,\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}}\vdash v_{1}^{\prime}\sim_{v}v^{\prime}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Action}\frac{}{{\begin{array}[]{l}\hat{\sigma}\sqsubseteq\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\,\wedge\,\hat{\sigma}\vdash\hat{v}\sim_{v}v\\
\hskip 18.49988pt\implies\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\vdash\hat{v}\sim_{v}v\end{array}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Weakening}

where
\hat{\sigma}\vdash\hat{\rho}\sim_{v}\rho
is shorthand for:
{\sf dom}(\hat{\rho})={\sf dom}(\rho)=X
and
\forall x\in X\,.\,\hat{\sigma}\vdash\hat{\rho}(x)\sim_{v}\rho(x)
.

Soundness Relations - Call stacks and Configurations

\hskip-2.27626pt\frac{}{\hat{\sigma}\vdash|f|\sim_{v}|f|}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Top Frame}\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{\hat{\sigma}\vdash\hat{\rho}\sim_{v}\rho\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\sigma}\vdash\hat{\mathit{cs}}\sim_{v}\mathit{cs}}{\hat{\sigma}\vdash|f,x,\hat{\rho},i|:\hat{\mathit{cs}}\sim_{v}|f,x,\rho,i|:\mathit{cs}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{Inner Frame}\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{\hat{\sigma}\sim_{s}\sigma\hat{\sigma}\vdash\hat{\mathit{cs}}\sim_{v}\mathit{cs}}{\langle\mathtt{p},\hat{\sigma},\hat{\mathit{cs}},i\rangle\sim_{s}\langle\mathtt{p},\sigma,\mathit{cs},i\rangle}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{$\sim_{s}$-Configuration}\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{\hat{\sigma}\leq\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}}{\langle\mathtt{p},\hat{\sigma},\hat{\mathit{cs}},i\rangle\leq\langle\mathtt{p},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime},\mathit{cs},i\rangle}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{$\leq$-Configuration}

Theorem B.2 (One-Step Soundness).

\mathit{SR}=\langle\downharpoonleft,\sim_{s},\sim_{v}\rangle

Theorem B.3 (Soundness - General).

Let
\mathit{SR}=\langle\downharpoonleft,\sim_{s},\sim_{v}\rangle
be a soundness relation
for
\hat{S}=\langle|\hat{S}|,\hat{V},A\rangle
in terms of
S=\langle|S|,V,A\rangle

and
\leq
the pre-order induced by
\sim_{s}
. It holds that:

\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{*}\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}\,\wedge\,\hat{c}\!f\leq\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}}\,\wedge\,\hat{c}\!f\sim_{s}c\!f\implies\exists\,c\!f^{\prime}\,.\,c\!f\rightsquigarrow^{*}c\!f^{\prime}\,\wedge\,\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f}\sim_{s}c\!f^{\prime}

Proof
: We proceed by induction on
n
.

Base Case:
n=0

1

(H1)
\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{0}\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}

2

(H2)
\hat{c}\!f\leq\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}}

3

(H3)
\hat{c}\!f\sim_{s}c\!f

\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}=\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}
[From H1]

c\!f\rightsquigarrow^{0}c\!f^{\prime}
[-]

To prove:
\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f}\sim_{s}c\!f^{\prime}

\hat{c}\!f\leq\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}}
[H2 + 1.2]

\hat{c}\!f\leq\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}
[1.4.1]

\hat{c}\!f\leq\hat{c}\!f

\hat{c}\!f\sqsubseteq\hat{c}\!f
[1.4.3]

\hat{c}\!f\leq\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}}
[1.4.2 + 1.4.4]

\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f}\sim_{s}c\!f
[1.4.5 + (H3)]

Inductive Step:
n=k+1

1

(H1)
\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{k+1}\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}

2

(H2)
\hat{c}\!f\leq\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}}

3

(H3)
\hat{c}\!f\sim_{s}c\!f
[From H1]

\exists\hat{c}\!f_{1}
:

1

\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{k}\hat{c}\!f_{1}
[To prove:
\hat{c}\!f_{1}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f}\leq\hat{c}\!f_{1}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}}
]

2

\hat{c}\!f_{1}\rightsquigarrow\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}

\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\hat{c}\!f_{1}
[From 2.2.2]

\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\leq\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}

\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}}\leq\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f_{1}}
[From 2.3 + 2.4]

\hat{c}\!f\leq\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f_{1}}
[From H2 + 2.5]

\exists c\!f^{\prime}
: [Applying the IH on H3 + 2.2.1 + 2.6]

1

c\!f\rightsquigarrow^{k}c\!f^{\prime}

2

\hat{c}\!f_{1}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f}\sim_{s}c\!f^{\prime\prime}

\hat{c}\!f_{1}\leq\hat{c}\!f_{1}
[-]

\hat{c}\!f\sqsubseteq\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}
[From H2]

\hat{c}\!f_{1}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f}\leq\hat{c}\!f_{1}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}}
[From 2.8 + 2.9]

\exists c\!f
: [Applying Theorem
3.2
to 2.2.1 + 2.7.1 + 2.10]

1

c\!f^{\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow c\!f

2

\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f}\sim_{s}c\!f^{\prime}

c\!f\rightsquigarrow^{k+1}c\!f^{\prime}
[From 2.7.1 + 2.11.1]

From there, by choosing
\hat{c}\!f\equiv\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}}
, we obtain the desired soundness result.

Corollary B.4 (Soundness).

Let
\mathit{SR}=\langle\downharpoonleft,\sim_{s},\sim_{v}\rangle
be a soundness relation
for
\hat{S}=\langle|\hat{S}|,\hat{V},A\rangle
in terms of
S=\langle|S|,V,A\rangle

and
\leq
the pre-order induced by
\sim_{s}
. It holds that:

\hat{c}\!f\rightsquigarrow^{*}\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\,\wedge\,(\hat{c}\!f\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}})\sim_{s}c\!f\implies\exists\,c\!f^{\prime}\,.\,c\!f\rightsquigarrow^{*}c\!f^{\prime}\,\wedge\,\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\sim_{s}c\!f^{\prime}

B.2.
Concrete-Symbolic Soundness

Definition B.5 (Symbolic Memory Interpretation).

Given a symbolic memory model
\hat{M}=\langle|\hat{M}|,A,\hat{{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{\lx@text@underline{ea}}\,}}\rangle

and a concrete memory model
M=\langle|M|,A,{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{\lx@text@underline{ea}}\,}\rangle
,
an interpretation of
\hat{M}
with respect to
M
is a function

\mathcal{I}:|\hat{M}|\rightarrow(\hat{\mathcal{X}}\rightharpoonup\mathcal{V})\rightharpoonup|M|

such that:

(8)

\begin{array}[]{l}\hat{\mu}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\alpha\,}(\hat{e},\pi)\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\mu}^{\prime},\hat{e}^{\prime},\pi^{\prime})\,\wedge\,\mu=\mathcal{I}(\hat{\mu},\varepsilon)\,\wedge\,\llbracket\pi\,\wedge\,\pi^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\varepsilon}={\sf true}\\
\qquad\qquad\implies\exists\,\mu^{\prime}\,.\,\mu^{\prime}=\mathcal{I}(\hat{\mu}^{\prime},\varepsilon)\,\wedge\,\mu.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\alpha\,}(\llbracket\hat{e}\rrbracket_{\varepsilon})=(\mu^{\prime},\llbracket\hat{e}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\varepsilon})\end{array}

Allocator Interpretation and Restriction.

Definition B.6 (Symbolic Allocator Interpretation).

Given a symbolic allocator model
\hat{A}L=\langle|\hat{A}L|,\hat{V}\rangle

and a concrete allocator model
AL=\langle|AL|,V\rangle
,
an interpretation of
\hat{A}L
with respect to
AL
is a function

\mathcal{I}_{AL}:|\hat{A}L|\rightarrow(\hat{V}\rightharpoonup V)\rightharpoonup|AL|

such that:

\begin{array}[]{l}\hat{\xi}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{alloc}\,}(j)\,\rightarrow_{Y}(\hat{\xi}^{\prime},\hat{v})\,\wedge\,\xi=\mathcal{I}_{AL}(\hat{\xi},\varepsilon)\implies\exists\,\xi^{\prime}.\,\xi^{\prime}=\mathcal{I}_{AL}(\hat{\xi}^{\prime},\varepsilon)\,\wedge\,\xi.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{alloc}\,}(j)\,\rightarrow_{\varepsilon(Y)}(\xi^{\prime},\varepsilon(\hat{v}))\end{array}

A restriction operator is said to be

preserved
by an allocator model
AL=\langle|AL|,V\rangle

if it satisfies the following two properties:

\frac{}{\xi.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{alloc}\,}(j)\,\rightarrow_{Y}(\xi^{\prime},v)\implies\xi^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\xi}\quad\textsc{RMono-Alloc}\qquad\frac{}{\xi.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{alloc}\,}(j)\,\rightarrow_{Y}(\xi^{\prime},v)\,\wedge\,\xi^{\prime\prime}\sqsubseteq\xi\!\downharpoonleft_{\xi^{\prime}}\\
\\
\implies\xi^{\prime\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{alloc}\,}(j)\,\rightarrow_{Y}(\xi^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\xi^{\prime\prime}},v)}\quad\textsc{FutureToPastAlloc}

We say that
\downharpoonleft
is a restriction operator on an allocator model

AL=\langle|AL|,V\rangle
, if
\downharpoonleft
is a restriction
operator on the carrier set
|AL|
and
\downharpoonleft
is preserved by
AL
.

Lifting Interpretations.

Given an interpretation
\mathcal{I}:|\hat{M}|\rightarrow(\hat{\mathcal{X}}\rightharpoonup\mathcal{V})\rightharpoonup|M|

of a symbolic memory model
\hat{M}=\langle|\hat{M}|,A,\hat{{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{\lx@text@underline{ea}}\,}}\rangle
in terms of
a concrete memory model
M=\langle|M|,A,{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{\lx@text@underline{ea}}\,}\rangle
,
the candidate soundness relation
RT(\mathcal{I})=\langle\downharpoonleft,\sim_{s},\sim_{v}\rangle
for

\mathit{SST}(\hat{M})
in terms of
\mathit{CST}(M)
is defined as follows:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi},\pi\rangle\!\downharpoonleft_{\langle\_,\_,\hat{\xi}^{\prime},\pi^{\prime}\rangle}&\triangleq&\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\xi}^{\prime}},\pi\,\wedge\,\pi^{\prime}\rangle\\
\hat{\sigma}\sim_{s}\sigma&\triangleq&\exists\,\varepsilon\,.\,(\sigma,\varepsilon)\in\mathcal{M}od(\hat{\sigma})\\
\langle\_,\_,\_,\pi\rangle\vdash\hat{e}\sim_{v}v&\triangleq&\exists\,\varepsilon\,.\,\llbracket\pi\rrbracket_{\varepsilon}={\sf true}\,\wedge\,\llbracket\hat{e}\rrbracket_{\varepsilon}=v\end{array}

where:

\mathcal{M}od(\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\pi,\hat{\xi}\rangle)\triangleq\left\{(\langle\mu,\rho,\xi\rangle,\varepsilon)\mid\llbracket\pi\rrbracket_{\varepsilon}={\sf true}\,\wedge\,\mu=\mathcal{I}(\hat{\mu},\varepsilon)\,\wedge\,\rho=\llbracket\hat{\rho}\rrbracket_{\varepsilon}\,\wedge\,\xi=\mathcal{I}_{AL}(\hat{\xi},\llbracket.\rrbracket_{\varepsilon})\right\}

Definition B.7 (Env).

The function
\mathtt{Env}:\Pi\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nv
is defined as follow :

\mathtt{Env}(\pi)\triangleq\left\{\varepsilon\mid\llbracket\pi\rrbracket_{\varepsilon}={\sf true}\right\}

If
\hat{\sigma}=\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi},\pi\rangle
, we also note

\mathtt{Env}(\hat{\sigma})=\mathtt{Env}(\pi)

Definition B.8 (Restriction of Interpretations with Path Conditions).

Given a path condition
\pi\in\Pi
, a concrete memory model
M\in\mathbb{M}
, a symbolic memory model
\hat{M}\in\hat{\mathbb{M}}
and an intepretation
\mathcal{I}::|\hat{M}|\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nv\rightharpoonup|M|
of
\hat{M}
with respect to
M
, we define

\mathcal{I}_{\pi}::|\hat{M}|\rightarrow\mathtt{Env}(\pi)\rightharpoonup|M|
, the restriction of
\mathcal{I}
to
\mathtt{Env}(\pi)
:

\mathcal{I}_{\pi}=\mathcal{I}\!\!\mid\!\mathtt{Env}(\pi)

Lemma B.9.

Let
M\in\mathbb{M}
and
\hat{M}\in\hat{\mathbb{M}}
be a concrete and a symbolic memory model and
\mathcal{I}::|\hat{M}|\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nv\rightharpoonup|M|
an interpretation of
\hat{M}
with respect to
M
, and let
\mathit{SR}=\langle\downharpoonleft,\sim_{s},\sim_{v}\rangle
be the candidate soundness relation between
\mathit{CST}(M)
and
\mathit{SST}(\hat{M})
induced by
\mathcal{I}
. It holds that :

\hat{\sigma}\sqsubseteq\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\iff\mathtt{Env}(\hat{\sigma})\subseteq\mathtt{Env}(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})\,\wedge\,\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}.\hat{\xi}\sqsubseteq\hat{\sigma}.\hat{\xi}

Proof
:

First, we need to observe that
\hat{\sigma}\sqsubseteq\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}
is just a shorthand for
\hat{\sigma}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}}=\hat{\sigma}
. We rewrite the equivalence we want to prove as

\hat{\sigma}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}}=\hat{\sigma}\iff\mathtt{Env}(\hat{\sigma})\subseteq\mathtt{Env}(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})\,\wedge\,\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}.\hat{\xi}\sqsubseteq\hat{\sigma}.\hat{\xi}

We analyse one direction of the equivalence at a time, first destructuring on
\hat{\sigma}
and
\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}
.

Let
\hat{\sigma}=\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi},\pi\rangle
and
\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}=\langle\hat{\mu}^{\prime},\hat{\rho}^{\prime},\hat{\xi}^{\prime},\pi^{\prime}\rangle

\hat{\sigma}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}}=\hat{\sigma}\implies\mathtt{Env}(\hat{\sigma})\subseteq\mathtt{Env}(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})\,\wedge\,\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}.\hat{\xi}\sqsubseteq\hat{\sigma}.\hat{\xi}

\hat{\sigma}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}}=\hat{\sigma}

\hat{\sigma}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}}=\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\xi}^{\prime}},\pi\land\pi^{\prime}\rangle
[From 1 and 2.1]

\pi\implies\pi^{\prime}
[From 2.1 and 2.2]

\hat{\xi}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\xi}^{\prime}}=\hat{\xi}
[From 2.1 and 2.2]

\mathtt{Env}(\hat{\sigma})\subseteq\mathtt{Env}(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})
[From 2.3]

\hat{\xi}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\hat{\xi}
[From 2.4]

\hat{\sigma}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}}=\hat{\sigma}\impliedby\mathtt{Env}(\hat{\sigma})\subseteq\mathtt{Env}(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})\,\wedge\,\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}.\hat{\xi}\sqsubseteq\hat{\sigma}.\hat{\xi}

1

\mathtt{Env}(\hat{\sigma})\subseteq\mathtt{Env}(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})

2

\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}.\hat{\xi}\sqsubseteq\hat{\sigma}.\hat{\xi}

\pi\implies\pi^{\prime}
[From 3.1.1]

\hat{\sigma}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}}=\hat{\sigma}
[From 3.1.2 and 3.2]

Lemma B.10.

Let
\hat{\sigma}=\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi},\pi\rangle
and
\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}=\langle\hat{\mu}^{\prime},\hat{\rho}^{\prime},\hat{\xi}^{\prime},\pi^{\prime}\rangle
. Then it holds that

\begin{array}[]{llll}&&&\mathcal{I}_{\pi}(\hat{\mu})=\mathcal{I}_{\pi}(\hat{\mu}^{\prime})\\
\hat{\sigma}\leq\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}&\iff&\,\wedge&\mathcal{I}_{\pi}(\hat{\rho})=\mathcal{I}_{\pi}(\hat{\rho}^{\prime})\\
&&\,\wedge&\pi\implies\pi^{\prime}\\
&&\,\wedge&\mathcal{I}_{\pi}(\hat{\xi}^{\prime})\sqsubseteq\mathcal{I}_{\pi}(\hat{\xi})\\
\end{array}

Lemma B.11.

Let
\hat{\sigma}=\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi},\pi\rangle
and
\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}=\langle\hat{\mu}^{\prime},\hat{\rho}^{\prime},\hat{\xi}^{\prime},\pi^{\prime}\rangle
. Then it holds that

\hat{\sigma}\sqsubseteq\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\iff(\pi\implies\pi^{\prime})\,\wedge\,\mathcal{I}_{\pi}(\hat{\xi}^{\prime})\sqsubseteq\mathcal{I}_{\pi}(\hat{\xi})

Lemma B.12 (Lifted Restriction Operator).

Let
\mathcal{I}
be an interpretation of a symbolic memory model
\hat{M}
in terms of a concrete memory model
M
, and
\langle\downharpoonleft,\sim_{s},\sim_{v}\rangle=RT(\mathcal{I})
. Then
\downharpoonleft
is a restriction operator on
\mathit{SST}(\hat{M})
.

Proof
:

Let
M\in\mathbb{M}
,
\hat{M}\in\hat{\mathbb{M}}
,
\mathcal{I}
an interpretation of
\hat{M}
in term of
M
,
\langle\downharpoonleft,\_,\_\rangle=RT(\mathcal{I})
.
We need to prove that
\downharpoonleft
is a restriction operator on the carrier set
|\mathit{SST}(\hat{M})|
, and that it is preserved by
\mathit{SST}(\hat{M})
.

\downharpoonleft
is a restriction operator

In order to prove that
\downharpoonleft
is a restriction operator, we need to prove its associativity, idempotence, right-commutativity and that it has the Weakening property.
We only provide the proof for associativity, the other proofs are analogous.

(\hat{\sigma}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}})\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}}=\hat{\sigma}\!\downharpoonleft_{(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}})}

1

\hat{\sigma}=\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi},\pi\rangle

2

\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}=\langle\hat{\mu}^{\prime},\hat{\rho}^{\prime},\hat{\xi}^{\prime},\pi^{\prime}\rangle

3

\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}=\langle\hat{\mu}^{\prime\prime},\hat{\rho}^{\prime\prime},\hat{\xi}^{\prime\prime},\pi^{\prime\prime}\rangle

\hat{\sigma}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}}=\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\xi}^{\prime}},\pi\land\pi^{\prime}\rangle

[From 1.1.1 and 1.1.2]

(\hat{\sigma}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}})\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}}=\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},(\hat{\xi}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\xi}^{\prime}})\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\xi}^{\prime\prime}},\pi\land\pi^{\prime}\land\pi^{\prime\prime}\rangle
[From 1.1.3 and1.2]

\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}}=\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi}^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\xi}^{\prime\prime}},\pi^{\prime}\land\pi^{\prime\prime}\rangle

[From 1.1.2 and 1.1.3]

\hat{\sigma}\!\downharpoonleft_{(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}})}=\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi}\!\downharpoonleft_{(\hat{\xi}^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\xi}^{\prime\prime}})},\pi^{\prime}\land\pi^{\prime\prime}\rangle

[From 1.1.1 and 1.4]

(\hat{\sigma}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}})\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}}=\hat{\sigma}\!\downharpoonleft_{(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}})}

[From 1.3, 1.5 and associativity of allocator restriction operator]

\downharpoonleft
is preserved by
\mathit{SST}(\hat{M})

In order to prove that
\downharpoonleft
is preserved by
\mathit{SST}(\hat{M})
, we need to prove that all of the state-generating functions exposed by the state model are monotonic with respect to the pre-order
\sqsubseteq
induced by
\downharpoonleft
. We only provide the proof for the monotonicity with respect to action executions, the other cases are analogous.

Actions

\hat{\sigma}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\alpha\,}(v)\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime},-)\implies\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\hat{\sigma}

\hat{\sigma}=\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi},\pi\rangle\in\mathit{SST}(\hat{M})

\hat{\sigma}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\alpha\,}(v)\rightsquigarrow(\sigma^{\prime},-)

\exists\hat{\mu}^{\prime},\pi^{\prime}\cdot

1

(\hat{\mu}^{\prime},\hat{e}^{\prime},\pi^{\prime})\in\hat{{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\textsf{\lx@text@underline{ea}}\,}}(\alpha,\hat{\mu},\hat{e})

2

\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}=\langle\hat{\mu}^{\prime},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi},\pi\,\wedge\,\pi^{\prime}\rangle

[From 2.2 and 2.3]

\mathtt{Env}(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})\subseteq\mathtt{Env}(\hat{\sigma})
[From 2.3.2]

\hat{\sigma}.\hat{\xi}\leq\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}.\hat{\xi}
[From 2.3.2]

\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\hat{\sigma}
[From 2.4, 2.5 and Lemma
B.9
]

Theorem B.13 (Soundness Relation - Lifting).

Let
\mathcal{I}
be an interpretation of a symbolic memory model
\hat{M}
in terms of
a concrete memory model
M
; then,
RT(\mathcal{I})=\langle\downharpoonleft,\sim_{s},\sim_{v}\rangle

is a soundness relation for
\mathit{SST}(\hat{M})
in terms of
\mathit{CST}(M)
.

Proof
:

In order to establish that
RT(\mathcal{I})=\langle\downharpoonleft,\sim_{s},\sim_{v}\rangle
is a soudness relation for
\mathit{SST}(\hat{M})
in terms of
\mathit{CST}(M)
, we need to prove that

1

\downharpoonleft
is a restriction order on
\mathit{SST}(\hat{M})

2

\sim_{s}
and
\sim_{v}
are monotonic with respect to the functions exposed by the state model

3

\downharpoonleft
is compatible with
\leq

The first property is the result of Lemma
B.12
. Let us prove property 2 and 3.

\sim_{s}
and
\sim_{v}
are monotonic with respect to the functions exposed by the state model

We need to prove that the property holds for

,
setStore
and actions. We only provide the proof for the action cases. The other cases are analogous.

\hat{\sigma}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\alpha\,}(\hat{v})\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime},\hat{v}^{\prime})\,\wedge\,\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}\leq\hat{\sigma}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}}\,\wedge\,\ \hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}\sim_{s}\sigma\,\wedge\,\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}\vdash\hat{v}\sim_{v}v\\
\qquad\implies\exists\,\sigma^{\prime},v^{\prime}\,.\,\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\alpha\,}(v)\rightsquigarrow(\sigma^{\prime},v^{\prime})\,\wedge\,\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}}\sim_{s}\sigma^{\prime}\,\wedge\,\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}}\vdash\hat{v}^{\prime}\sim_{v}v^{\prime}

(H1)
\hat{\sigma}=\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi},\pi\rangle

\exists\hat{\mu}^{\prime},\hat{v}^{\prime},\pi^{\prime}\cdot

\hat{\sigma}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\alpha\,}(\hat{v})\rightsquigarrow\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi},\pi\,\wedge\,\pi^{\prime}\rangle,\hat{v}^{\prime})

3

\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}=\langle\hat{\mu},\hat{\rho},\hat{\xi},\pi\,\wedge\,\pi^{\prime}\rangle

[From H1 and H4]

\exists\varepsilon\cdot

[From H6, 1.3.1 ]

[From H1 and 1.2.3]

1

[From H3, 1.10.3 and 1.11]

B.3.

The interpretation of While symbolic memories in terms of While concrete memories,
\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{W}}
,
is inductively defined as follows:

\frac{}{\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{W}}(\emptyset,\varepsilon)\triangleq\emptyset}\quad\textsc{Empty}\frac{l=\llbracket\hat{e}\rrbracket_{\varepsilon}v=\llbracket\hat{e}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\varepsilon}}{\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{W}}(\hat{e}.p\mapsto\hat{e}^{\prime},\varepsilon)\triangleq l.p\mapsto v}\quad\textsc{Cell}\frac{\mu_{1}=\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{W}}(\hat{\mu}_{1},\varepsilon)\mu_{2}=\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{W}}(\hat{\mu}_{2},\varepsilon)}{\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{W}}(\hat{\mu}_{1}\uplus\hat{\mu}_{2},\varepsilon)\triangleq\mu_{1}\uplus\mu_{2}}\quad\textsc{Union}

is an interpretation of

Definition C.1 (Predicate State Constructor (
\mathit{PST}
)).

The predicate state constructor
\mathit{PST}:\mathbb{S}\rightarrow\mathbb{S}
is defined as

\mathit{PST}(\langle|S|,V,A\rangle)\triangleq\langle|S^{\prime}|,V,A\uplus\{{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setP}\,}\!,{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{getP}\,}\!\}\rangle
,
where:

\bullet

|S^{\prime}|

\triangleq

|S|\times\overline{\Omega_{V}}

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setVar}\,}\!_{{\sf p}}(\langle\sigma,\overline{\omega}\rangle,x,v)

\triangleq

\langle{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setVar}\,}\!(\sigma,x,v),\overline{\omega}\rangle

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setStore}\,}\!_{{\sf p}}(\langle\sigma,\overline{\omega}\rangle,\rho)

\triangleq

\langle{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setStore}\,}\!(\sigma,\rho),\overline{\omega}\rangle

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{store}\,}\!_{{\sf p}}(\langle\sigma,-\rangle)

\triangleq

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{store}\,}\!(\sigma)

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ee}\,}\!_{{\sf p}}(\langle\sigma,-\rangle,e)

\triangleq

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ee}\,}\!(\sigma,e)

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ea}\,}\!_{{\sf p}}(\alpha,\langle\sigma,\overline{\omega}\rangle,v)

\triangleq

\{(\langle\sigma^{\prime},\overline{\omega}\rangle,v^{\prime})\mid(\sigma^{\prime},v^{\prime})\in{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ea}\,}\!(\alpha,\sigma,v)\}
,
if

\alpha\not\in\{{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{getP}\,}\!,{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setP}\,}\!\}

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ea}\,}\!_{{\sf p}}({\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setP}\,}\!,\langle\sigma,\overline{\omega}\rangle,\left[\mathit{pn},v\right])

\triangleq

(\langle\sigma,(\mathit{pn}(v):\overline{\omega})\rangle,-)

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ea}\,}\!_{{\sf p}}({\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{getP}\,}\!,\langle\sigma,\overline{\omega}\rangle,\left[\mathit{pn},v\right])

\triangleq

(\langle\sigma,\overline{\omega}_{1}+\kern-5.03749pt+\kern 3.09999pt\overline{\omega}_{2}\rangle,-)
,
where
\overline{\omega}=\overline{\omega}_{1}+\kern-5.03749pt+\kern 3.09999pt\left[\,\mathit{pn}(v)\,\right]+\kern-5.03749pt+\kern 3.09999pt\overline{\omega}_{2}

Definition C.2 (Core Predicate Action Interpretation).

A core predicate action interpretation is a 4-tuple
AI\in\mathcal{A\mkern-1.0muI}=\langle\Delta,A,set,get\rangle

consisting of a set of core predicates
\Delta
, a set of actions
A
, and two functions

set,get:\Delta\rightarrow A
; we write
{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{set}\,}\!_{\delta}
for
set(\delta)
and
{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{get}\,}\!_{\delta}
for
get(\delta)
.
A core predicate action interpretation
\langle\Delta,A,set,get\rangle
is said to be
well-formed

with respect to a state model

S=\langle|S|,V,A\rangle
in and only if,
for all core predicates
\delta\in\Delta
, it holds that:

(10)

\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{get}\,}\!_{\delta}\,}(v)\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime}\iff\sigma^{\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{set}\,}\!_{\delta}\,}(v)\rightsquigarrow\sigma

Given a state model
S=\langle|S|,V,A\rangle
and a core action
interpretation
\langle\Delta,A,set,get\rangle
, the induced action interpretation of an
assertion
P\in\mathcal{A}_{\Delta}
is a pair of functions consisting of the

getter
and
setter
of
P
, respectively,
{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{get}\,}\!_{P}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{set}\,}\!_{P}
.
Formally, we define two induced functions:

•

set_{\Delta}^{S}:\mathcal{A}_{\Delta}\rightarrow|S|\rightarrow(\hat{\mathcal{X}}\uplus\mathcal{X}\rightharpoonup V)\rightharpoonup|S|

(
\sigma^{\prime}=set_{\Delta}^{S}(P,\sigma,\theta)\equiv_{pp}\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{set}\,}\!_{P}(\theta)\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime}
)

•

get_{\Delta}^{S}:\mathcal{A}_{\Delta}\rightarrow|S|\rightarrow(\hat{\mathcal{X}}\uplus\mathcal{X}\rightharpoonup V)\rightharpoonup|S|

(
\sigma^{\prime}=get_{\Delta}^{S}(P,\sigma,\theta)\equiv_{pp}\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{get}\,}\!_{P}(\theta)\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime}
)

mapping each assertion
P\in\mathcal{A}_{\Delta}
to its getter and setter, respectively, as follows:

Assertion Interpretation:
\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{set}\,}\!_{P}(\theta)\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime}
and
\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{get}\,}\!_{P}(\theta)\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime}

\frac{\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{set}\,}\!_{P}(\theta)\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime}\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\sigma^{\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{set}\,}\!_{Q}(\theta)\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime\prime}}{\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{set}\,}\!_{(P\ast Q)}(\theta)\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{set - Lemma C.3 (Assertion Interpretation).

Let
\langle\Delta,A,set,get\rangle
be a well-formed core predicate interpretation
with respect to a predicate state model
S=\langle|S|,V,A\rangle
; then, it holds that:

\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{set}\,}\!_{P}(\theta)\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime}
if and only if

\sigma^{\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{get}\,}\!_{P}(\theta)\rightsquigarrow\sigma
.

Theorem C.4 (Assertion Interpretation - Soundness).

Let
\mathit{SR}=\langle\downharpoonleft,\sim_{s},\sim_{v}\rangle
be a soundness relation
for
\hat{S}=\langle|\hat{S}|,\hat{V},A\rangle
in terms of

Theorem C.5 (Verification).

Let
\mathit{SR}=\langle\downharpoonleft,\sim_{s},\sim_{v}\rangle
be a soundness relation
for
\hat{S}=\langle|\hat{S}|,\hat{V},A\rangle
in terms of
S=\langle|S|,V,A\rangle

and
\leq
the pre-order induced by
\sim_{s}
. It holds that:

\hat{c}\!f~\underline{\rightsquigarrow}^{*}~\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\,\wedge\,\hat{c}\!f=\hat{c}\!f\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}}\,\wedge\,\hat{c}\!f\sim_{s}c\!f\implies\exists\,c\!f^{\prime}\,.\,c\!f~\underline{\rightsquigarrow}^{*}~c\!f^{\prime}\,\wedge\,\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\sim_{s}c\!f^{\prime}

Proof
:

In order to prove this theorem, we prove the following more general claim, from which the theorem immediately follows :

\hat{c}\!f~\underline{\rightsquigarrow}^{*}~\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\,\wedge\,\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}\leq\hat{c}\!f\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}}\,\wedge\,\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}\sim_{s}c\!f\implies\exists\,c\!f^{\prime}\,.\,c\!f~\underline{\rightsquigarrow}^{*}~c\!f^{\prime}\,\wedge\,\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}}\sim_{s}c\!f^{\prime}

We proceed by use analysis on the rule used to derive
\hat{c}\!f~\underline{\rightsquigarrow}^{*}~\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}
. We only cover the non-logic command, unfold and spec call cases, the other cases being analogous.

Non-logic commands

1

(H1)
\hat{c}\!f~\underline{\rightsquigarrow}^{*}~\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}

2

(H2)
\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}\leq\hat{c}\!f\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}}

3

(H3)
\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}\sim_{s}c\!f

\exists\,c\!f^{\prime}\,.\,c\!f~\underline{\rightsquigarrow}^{*}~c\!f^{\prime}\,\wedge\,\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\sim_{s}c\!f^{\prime}

\hat{c}\!f~\rightsquigarrow~\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}
[From H1]

\exists c\!f^{\prime}.

1

c\!f\rightsquigarrow c\!f^{\prime}

2

\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}}\rightsquigarrow c\!f^{\prime}

[From H2, H3, 1.2 and Theorem
4.1
]

c\!f~\underline{\rightsquigarrow}^{*}c\!f^{\prime}
[From 1.3 and execution of non-logic command.]

Unfold

1

(H1)
\hat{c}\!f~\underline{\rightsquigarrow}^{*}~\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}

2

(H2)
\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}=\hat{c}\!f\!\downharpoonleft_{\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}}

3

(H3)
\hat{c}\!f^{\prime\prime}\sim_{s}c\!f

\exists\,c\!f^{\prime}\,.\,c\!f~\underline{\rightsquigarrow}^{*}~c\!f^{\prime}\,\wedge\,\hat{c}\!f^{\prime}\sim_{s}c\!f^{\prime}

\exists\,\underline{\mathtt{p}},\hat{\sigma},\hat{\sigma}_{1},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime},\mathit{cs}_{1},i,j,\mathit{pn},e,x,{P_{i}}\!\mid_{i=0}^{n},\hat{v}.

1

\hat{c}\!f=\langle\hat{\sigma},\mathit{cs}_{1},i\rangle

2

Lemma C.6 (While: Concrete Core Predicate Interpretation).

The core predicate action interpretation
\langle\Delta_{\mathbb{W}},A_{\mathbb{W}},[\underline{\sf cell}\mapsto\underline{\sf setCell}],[\underline{\sf cell}\mapsto\underline{\sf getCell}]\rangle
is well-formed
with respect to
\mathit{CST}(M_{\mathbb{W}})
.

Proof
:

We need to prove that the following equivalent holds for any concrete memories
\mu,\mu^{\prime}\in M_{\mathbb{W}}
, and any triple
\left[l,p,v\right]
:

\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\underline{\sf getCell}\,}(\left[l,p,v\right])\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime}\iff\sigma^{\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\underline{\sf setCell}\,}(\left[l,p,v\right])\rightsquigarrow\sigma

We start by proving the left to right implication, before proving the right to left implication.

\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\underline{\sf getCell}\,}(\left[l,p,v\right])\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime}\Rightarrow\sigma^{\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\underline{\sf setCell}\,}(\left[l,p,v\right])\rightsquigarrow\sigma

(H1)
\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\underline{\sf getCell}\,}(\left[l,p,v\right])\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime}

\sigma^{\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\underline{\sf setCell}\,}(\left[l,p,v\right])\rightsquigarrow\sigma

\exists\,\mu^{\prime}.\mu=\mu^{\prime}\uplus(l,p)\mapsto v
[From (H1)]

\exists\,\mu^{\prime\prime},p_{i}\!\mid_{i=1}^{n}.

1

\mu^{\prime}\!\upharpoonright_{l}=\mu^{\prime\prime}\uplus\_

2

\mu^{\prime\prime}=\uplus_{i=1}^{n}(l,p_{i})\mapsto\_

3

(p_{i}\neq p)\mid_{i=1}^{n}

[From 1.2]

\sigma^{\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\underline{\sf setCell}\,}(\left[l,p,v\right])\rightsquigarrow\sigma
[From 1.3]

\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\underline{\sf getCell}\,}(\left[l,p,v\right])\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime}\Leftarrow\sigma^{\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\underline{\sf setCell}\,}(\left[l,p,v\right])\rightsquigarrow\sigma

(H1)
\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\underline{\sf setCell}\,}(\left[l,p,v\right])\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime}

\sigma^{\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\underline{\sf getCell}\,}(\left[l,p,v\right])\rightsquigarrow\sigma

\exists\,\mu^{\prime\prime},p_{i}\!\mid_{i=1}^{n}.

[From 2.2]

[From 2.2]

Lemma C.8 (Extended While: Memory Interpretation).

\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{W}}
is an intepretation of While symbolic memories
\hat{M}_{\mathbb{W}}
with respect to the concrete memories
M_{\mathbb{W}}
, for While extended with specifications.

Proof
:

For every action
\alpha\in A_{\mathbb{W}}
, we have to prove that:

\forall\hat{\mu},\hat{\mu}^{\prime}\in\hat{M}_{\mathbb{W}};\,\mu\in M_{\mathbb{W}};\,\hat{e},\hat{e}^{\prime}\in\hat{\mathcal{E}};\,\varepsilon\in\mathcal{E}nv;\,\pi,\pi^{\prime}\in\Pi
.

(15)

\begin{array}[]{l}\hat{\mu}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\alpha\,}(\hat{e},\pi)\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\mu}^{\prime},\hat{e}^{\prime},\pi^{\prime})\,\wedge\,\mu=\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{W}}(\hat{\mu},\varepsilon)\,\wedge\,\llbracket\pi\,\wedge\,\pi^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\varepsilon}={\sf true}\\

Section 5: Parametric Bi-Abductive Analysis

D.1.
Parametric Bi-abductive Analysis

Action Fixes

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ea}\,}\!:A\rightarrow|S|\rightarrow V\rightharpoonup\wp(|S|\times V)\uplus\wp(\mathcal{A}_{\Delta})

(
(\sigma^{\prime},v^{\prime})\in{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ea}\,}\!(\alpha,\sigma,v)\equiv_{pp}\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\alpha\,}(v)\rightsquigarrow{\mathtt{S}}\langle\sigma^{\prime},v^{\prime}\rangle
)

(
P\in{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ea}\,}\!(\alpha,\sigma,v)\equiv_{pp}\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\alpha\,}(v)\rightsquigarrow{\mathtt{F}}\langle P\rangle
)

Definition D.1 (Well-formedness of Fixes).

If the original
state is extended with a generated fix, it must be possible to execute the action successfully.

(16)

\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\alpha\,}(v)\rightsquigarrow{\mathtt{F}}\langle P\rangle\,\wedge\,\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{set}\,}\!_{P}([~])\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime\prime}\implies\exists\,\sigma^{\prime},v^{\prime}.\sigma^{\prime\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\alpha\,}(v)\rightsquigarrow{\mathtt{S}}\langle\sigma^{\prime},v^{\prime}\rangle

Bi-Abductive Analysis.

Definition D.2 (BiState Constructor (
\mathit{BiST}
)).

The bi-abductive state constructor
\mathit{BiST}:\mathbb{S}\rightarrow\mathbb{S}
is defined as

\mathit{BiST}(\langle|S|,V,A\rangle)\triangleq\langle|S^{\prime}|,V,A\rangle
,
where:

\bullet

|S^{\prime}|

\triangleq

|S|\times\mathcal{A}_{\Delta}

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setVar}\,}\!_{{\sf bi}}(\langle\sigma,P\rangle,x,v)

\triangleq

\langle{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setVar}\,}\!(\sigma,x,v),P\rangle

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setStore}\,}\!_{{\sf bi}}(\langle\sigma,P\rangle,\rho)

\triangleq

\langle{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setStore}\,}\!(\sigma,\rho),P\rangle

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{store}\,}\!_{{\sf bi}}(\langle\sigma,-,-\rangle)

\triangleq

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{store}\,}\!(\sigma)

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ee}\,}\!_{{\sf bi}}(\langle\sigma,-,-\rangle,e)

\triangleq

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ee}\,}\!(\sigma,e)

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ea}\,}\!_{{\sf bi}}(\alpha,\langle\sigma,P\rangle,v)

\triangleq

\left\{(\langle\sigma^{\prime},P\rangle,v^{\prime})\mid\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\alpha\,}(v)\rightsquigarrow{\mathtt{S}}\langle\sigma^{\prime},v^{\prime}\rangle\right\}
if
\alpha\neq{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{assume}\,}\!

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ea}\,}\!_{{\sf bi}}({\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{assume}\,}\!,\langle\sigma,P\rangle,v)

\triangleq

\left\{(\langle\sigma^{\prime},P*\pi\rangle,v^{\prime})\mid\lceil v\rceil=\pi\,\wedge\,\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{assume}\,}(v)\,\rightsquigarrow{\mathtt{S}}\langle\sigma^{\prime},v^{\prime}\rangle\right\}

\bullet

{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ea}\,}\!_{{\sf bi}}(\alpha,\langle\sigma,P\rangle,v)

\triangleq

\left\{{\begin{array}[]{l}\langle\sigma^{\prime\prime},P*Q\rangle,v^{\prime})\\
\hskip 18.49988pt\mid\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\alpha\,}(v)\rightsquigarrow{\mathtt{F}}\langle Q\rangle\,\wedge\,\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{set}\,}\!_{Q}([~])\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime}\,\wedge\,\sigma^{\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\alpha\,}(v)\rightsquigarrow{\mathtt{S}}\langle\sigma^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime}\rangle\end{array}}\right\}

Lemma D.3 (One-Step).

\begin{array}[]{l}\underline{\mathtt{p}}\vdash\langle\langle\sigma,P\rangle,\mathit{cs},i\rangle\,\underline{\rightsquigarrow}_{\mathit{BiST}(S)}\langle\langle\sigma^{\prime},Q\rangle,\mathit{cs}^{\prime},j\rangle^{o}\\
\qquad\implies\exists\,P^{\prime}.\,Q\vdash P\ast P^{\prime}\,\wedge\,\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{set}\,}\!_{P^{\prime}}(\left[\right])\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime\prime}\,\wedge\,\sigma^{\prime\prime}\leq\sigma\!\downharpoonleft_{\sigma^{\prime}}\underline{\mathtt{p}}\vdash\langle\sigma^{\prime\prime},\mathit{cs},i\rangle\,\underline{\rightsquigarrow}_{S}\langle\sigma^{\prime},\mathit{cs}^{\prime},j\rangle^{o}\end{array}

Proof
:

We proceed by case analysis on the rule used to produce
\underline{\mathtt{p}}\vdash\langle\langle\sigma,P\rangle,\mathit{cs},i\rangle\,\underline{\rightsquigarrow}_{\mathit{BiST}(S)}\langle\langle\sigma^{\prime},Q\rangle,\mathit{cs}^{\prime},j\rangle^{o}
.

We only provide the proof for the execute action case. The other cases are analogous.

There are two rules concerning the execute action case :

1

The case where the action does not generate an error

2

The case where the action generates an error.
We are providing the proof for both cases.

[No Error]

\underline{\mathtt{p}}\vdash\langle\langle\sigma,P\rangle,\mathit{cs},i\rangle\,\underline{\rightsquigarrow}_{\mathit{BiST}(S)}\langle\langle\sigma^{\prime},Q\rangle,\mathit{cs}^{\prime},j\rangle^{o}

1

{\sf cmd}(\underline{\mathtt{p}},\mathit{cs},i)=x:=\alpha(\exp)

2

\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ee}\,}(\exp)=v

3

\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\alpha\,}(v)\rightsquigarrow{\mathtt{S}}\langle\sigma^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime}\rangle

4

\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma^{\prime\prime}.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{setVar}\,}\!(x,v^{\prime})

[From 1.1]

\underline{\mathtt{p}}\vdash\langle\sigma,\mathit{cs},i\rangle\,\underline{\rightsquigarrow}_{S}\langle\sigma^{\prime},\mathit{cs},i+1\rangle^{o}

[From 1.2]

P\vdash P\ast{\sf emp}

\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{set}\,}\!_{{\sf emp}}(\left[\right])\rightsquigarrow\sigma

[Action Error]

\underline{\mathtt{p}}\vdash\langle\langle\sigma,P\rangle,\mathit{cs},i\rangle\,\underline{\rightsquigarrow}_{\mathit{BiST}(S)}\langle\langle\sigma^{\prime},Q\rangle,\mathit{cs}^{\prime},j\rangle^{o}

1

{\sf cmd}(\underline{\mathtt{p}},\mathit{cs},i)=x:=\alpha(\exp)

\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{ee}\,}(\exp)=v

Theorem D.4 (Multiple-Step).

\begin{array}[]{l}\underline{\mathtt{p}}\vdash\langle\langle\sigma,P\rangle,\mathit{cs},i\rangle\,\underline{\rightsquigarrow}_{\mathit{BiST}(S)}^{*}\langle\langle\sigma^{\prime},Q\rangle,\mathit{cs}^{\prime},j\rangle^{o}\\
\qquad\implies\exists\,P^{\prime}.\,Q\vdash P\ast P^{\prime}\,\wedge\,\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{set}\,}\!_{P^{\prime}}(\left[\right])\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime\prime}\,\wedge\,\sigma^{\prime\prime}\leq\sigma\!\downharpoonleft_{\sigma^{\prime}}\underline{\mathtt{p}}\vdash\langle\sigma^{\prime\prime},\mathit{cs},i\rangle\,\underline{\rightsquigarrow}_{S}^{*}\langle\sigma^{\prime},\mathit{cs}^{\prime},j\rangle^{o}\end{array}

.

Theorem D.5 (Bi-Abduction).

\begin{array}[]{l}\underline{\mathtt{p}}\vdash\langle\langle\sigma,{\sf emp}\rangle,\mathit{cs},i\rangle\,\underline{\rightsquigarrow}_{\mathit{BiST}(S)}^{*}\langle\langle\sigma^{\prime},P\rangle,\mathit{cs}^{\prime},j\rangle^{o}\,\wedge\,\sigma.{\color[rgb]{0.33,0.34,0.32}\text{set}\,}\!_{P}([~])\rightsquigarrow\sigma^{\prime\prime}\\
